Action! — Pay-per-view

Juggernaut II, 25 May 2003

25 May 2003
Content noticeA period document, served unedited. It carries the humor, language, and content typical of its era and community — some of it crude, dated, or offensive, including material that wouldn't be written today. See Curation.
Juggernaut II Madison Square Garden — New York City, New York ---- JUGGERNAUT II. JUGGERNAUT II. Introduction. In light of my present mess In light of my mental weather's turn In light of all the crash and burn I'll open my mind as wide as ever The line behind the lesson Soul in the open well It might have been fate It might have been fortune It might have been wine and a mellow head I'm finding my way Finding the words One body breaking Someone come and rise up Rise like the sun You'll find the world Somebody save me Someone come and rise up Rise up and hold me now I know that at times life kinda hurts I know that I will live and learn So more than survive I will endeavor The mind behind the message Alone on an ocean swell It might have been great It might have gorgeous It might have been why I've settled in I'm finding my way Finding the words One body breaking Someone come and rise up Rise like the sun You'll find the world Somebody save me Someone come and rise up Rise and show A helpless man A held out hand Defining images That flow through my hope Surrender the broken moments And pointing fingers The closing of open minds I might have been delayed I might have been delirious I might have been otherwise Taking hammer blows Like an animal For others So heavy was my way I'm finding my way Finding the words One body breaking Someone come and rise up Rise like the sun You'll find the world Somebody save me Someone come and rise up I'm finding my way I'm finding my words I'm one body breaking Someone come and rise up Rise like the sun You'll find the world Somebody save me Someone come and rise up Rise and show Show us Something “Like the Sun” - I Mother Earth ---- Sharc vs. Fiend vs. Kayden Paulton Sharc vs. Fiend vs. Kayden Paulton The wrestling portion of Juggernaut II was ready to begin. "Come To Daddy" began playing, bringing the New Yorkers to their feet, though not necessarily in cheers. It was time for the triple threat match, and out stepped the first participant. Fiend. The 6-foot-3, 255 pound man paused at the top of the ramp with a determined look on his face. He bouncing his head from one shoulder to the other before walking to the ring. Once on the apron, he stared at the fans in the sold-out arena before cracking his knuckles and getting into the ring. Ump: With what these three have been doing to each other in the last few weeks, I would expect nothing but an amazing effort by Sharc, Paulton and Fiend in this contest. Shaw: It's too bad they all made it to this thing. We could have made the night just a tad bit shorter. Ump: I thought size was everything. Shaw: _ Ump: This night is going to be huge. Shaw: Yeah, well_. But before Shaw could make up for his 'little' verbal mistake, the fans exploded in cheers for one of the most likable people in this business: Kayden Paulton. "96 Quite Bitter Beings" hit and out came Kayden Paulton, the big first-face pop of the night. But he has earned it in his Action career. This night could get no bigger for him. Win, lose, draw, count-out, DQ, whatever. He was at Juggernaut II! And in his face, you could see it. He nearly bounced down to the ring, slapping some lucky fans hands as he went. While his song was "96 Quite Bigger Beings," Paulton, the 6-foot-4, 266 pounder, was about to meet two bitter beings in this match. One of them was already in the ring, and Fiend snarled as Paulton waved at him and yelled a "Hi Fiend!" Fiend was just waiting for him to get into the ring so he could beat on him. And that's just what happened. Ump: Fiend is going right after Paulton, dragging him over the top rope by his neck and slamming him hard to the mat. Fiend: Fiend, I could have gotten in myself, but thanks! Shaw: That kid just doesn't no when to shut up, does he? Ump: Fiend drags Paulton to the corner. Paulton: WHEEEE! Shaw: *Sigh* That kid is WAY to happy to be in this match. And now he's getting stomped down. And I bet he's enjoying THAT too. Ump: The bell hasn't yet sounded because the third man hasn't been introduced yet. Then it hit. "Disciple." The fans looked toward the entryway, as the lights went blood red. That didn't stop Fiend from whipping Paulton into the opposite corner and following it up with a running clothesline into a shoulder rack to modified backbreaker. That's when Sharc stepped out, holding his barbed wire 2x4, and followed closely by his valet, Bait. He didn't rush at all going to the ring, simply looking at the scene as Fiend waited on Sharc, while Paulton began to push himself up. Sharc, at 6-2, 245 pounds, was smaller than both of his opponents. But the one thing he didn't have was fear, which is why he charged straight into the ring, not even dropping his weapon, and being greeted by several stomps to the back of his head. Ump: Fiend is dominating here early, now picking up Sharc. And the bell sounds now that both men are in the ring. Shaw: Well, not for long. Ump: Oh hell. Fiend has Sharc up for a Gorilla press slam! He looks like he's ready to do some Sharc tossing, out to the floor! Shaw: He better not be looking at OUR table! Ump: Or the French table. French announcer: Ce grand idiot meilleur de ne pas le jeter ici! Cette table est réservée une rupture plus tard! Fiend est stupide. Ump: Thank God we don't have to speak French. Shaw: Thank god we don't have to BE French. Idiots. Now he's eyeing the Spanish table? Spanish announcer: ¡Láncelo en las ranas! ¡Déjenos solos! ¡Apoyamos la guerra con Iraq! ¡Honesto! Shaw: God bless Babelfish. Let me see what those foreigners said. Oh no! Damn AOL! Ump: There IS a match going on. Shaw: If I ever try to buy a Mac again, kill me. Ump: Fiend finally dumps Sharc in the ring. But only because Kayden just clipped Fiend's knee from behind. But Fiend is back up. Oh! Nice superkick by Paulton. There's the cover. One, two, Sharc with a leg drop on the back of Paulton's head. Sharc picks up Paulton and shoves him into the corner face-first. Shaw: If Kayden could speak, he'd probably thank Sharc for this one. Ump: Sharc jams his shoulder into Paulton's lower back. Oh, how brutal. And again, and again. Shaw: Here comes Fiend. Ump: Sharc turns around and swings, but Fiend blocks. Fiend swings but Sharc blocks it. Both men are locked up. Vicious head butt by Fiend. Fiend with an underhook belly to belly suplex. And a cover. One, kickout before two. Shaw: That was Kayden Paulton's Repetition of History Move, pretty much. And he did it bunches better. Ump: In your mind. Paulton is up and bulldogs Fiend from behind. He makes the cover. He gets just barely a two there. Paulton puts Sharc into a headlock. Fiend gets up again, and Paulton gets his feet around his head. Wow! Nice move there by Paulton, taking both men down, and Paulton hangs onto Sharc in the headlock. Shaw: Why would the ref bother checking on Sharc, he hasn't quit once in his career. Pinned, plenty of times, but he has never submitted. A headlock isn't going to do sh-- Ump: Sharc is fighting his way up. And he counters with a simple belly to back suplex. Paulton hung on! Shaw: He did? How did he do that? Ump: I don't know, but here comes Fiend. Good lord, Paulton just tripped him. Fiend's throat went right onto the middle rope there, and now he's gasping for some breath. Paulton: Ooops! Sorry, Fiend. Didn't see you there. Shaw: That was unintentional? Ump: Sharc's fought his way up again. He's got him up for another belly to back suplex. NO! Oh now, he just crotched Paulton on the top rope! Sharc grabs Fiend and rams Fiend's head into Paulton's. Paulton topples to the apron and down to the floor. Shaw: A meeting of the mindless, right there. Ump: Sharc hits a Russian leg sweep on a dazed Fiend and goes for the cover. Fiend gets a shoulder up just as the two comes down. Now Sharc tosses Fiend to the floor in frustration. All three fighters are on the floor, while Sharc is up. Shaw: The ref better not count these guys out if he doesn't want a riot on his hands. A count out in the first match? Think about it. Ump: Sharc has Paulton up. Oh no. NO! He just crotched Paulton into the steel post. And he follows that up with a reverse DDT to the floor. Shaw: He's in his element on the floor. Anywhere but IN the ring, it seems. Ump: Fiend is up and Sharc ducks a clothesline. Sharc with a blatant kick to the crotch. DDT on Fiend on the floor. Shaw: Hey, look, Bait just went into the ring. Well, she just broke up the referee's count there. Bait has him backed up into the corner. And her back is to him. What the? Ump: That's getting a nice pop from the crowd. Shaw: And no doubt from the referee! Ump: How do you call this? Shaw: Umm, Bait is pretending to give him a standing up lap dance? Ump: You called it. Meanwhile, Sharc has retrieved his barbed wire board and is now choking Fiend with the wood. I don't expect Sharc to wait too long to bleed Fiend with the wire. Paulton: Sharc_you really shouldn't choke him. Ump: Sharc just dropped Fiend. Sharc charges Paulton, backdrop! Paulton goes for the barbed wire board. Don't tell me he's going to use it on Sharc. Shaw: Bait just came out to the floor with the referee. Bait yanks the board from his grip. She swings at Paulton. He ducks! Ump: Bait just hit Sharc the top of his head with the barbed wire! Sharc falls in a heap back to the floor. Bait just slapped Paulton. Fiend charges at Paulton, who ducks again! Bait goes down in a heap! The crowd erupted on that one, with a "Ohhhhh." Ump: Fiend looks down in indifference after taking out the woman with a clothesline, and now goes after Paulton, who just rolled back into the ring. Paulton springs off the ropes. Crossbody block and a cover. He has him down, but the referee is checking on Bait. Fiend is yelling! He gets into the ring for_a one-count. Shaw: Sharc is busted. Sharc looks down at Bait in shock. Fiend takes Paulton with a Samoan drop. Sharc picks Bait up and is taking her up the ramp? Did he forget there is a match going on? Ump: Fiend just saw that and heads out to the floor. He's going after Sharc in a sprint. Sharc turned around just in time to see Fiend charging and sidestep him. *BOOM* Ump: Fiend just speared the stage! And collapses. Sharc hands off Bait to one of the referees. And he's got fire in his eyes. He drags Fiend up. *THUD* Ump: And rams his face into the stage. *THUD THUD THUD THUD THUD THUD* Crowd: Eight! Nine! TEN! The crowd cheered at Fiend's expense, after having his face rammed into the side of the stage ten times. Ump: Sharc is heading back down to the ring. Paulton: Aww, c'mon ref. Let them come back. Can't we forget the count outs? Ump: And Sharc just stopped dead in his tracks. Shaw: He's going back for Fiend. Ump: He's got Fiend, whose face is covered in a mask of blood. He pulls him up onto the top of the stage. Oh no, way. He's trying for a powerbomb? Shaw: Maybe a piledriver on the steel stage. There is no give up there, as you fans should know. Not that there is much more give in the ring. It wouldn't take much to hurt Fiend's neck on steel. Ump: What the? Sharc just tried to sunset flip him to the floor! Fiend somehow, is holding on, avoiding what could easily put him on the shelf for months, or longer. Shaw: Fiend grabs Sharc by the throat. Uh oh. A two-handed chokeslam coming up. Ump: Oh, but Sharc, counters, if you want to call it that, with a jab to Fiend's eye. It was just enough to get Sharc free, who is running back up the aisle. Shaw: What is he doing? Ump: He just shoved a fan to the ground. Oh no. He just took one of those special Juggernaut chairs that the fans can take home. He's running back toward Fiend! *CRACK* *CRACK* *CRACK* The crowd roared in shock at each of the blasts, letting out roars and "ohhhs" with every blast. Ump: Welcome to Juggernaut II! Shaw: Indeed. Ump: Sharc drops the bloody chair and is now staggering back to the ring. Paulton is holding the ropes open for Sharc to come in. Shaw: Did Sharc just say "thank you"? Ump: He's getting in with him. Oh no he didn't. He just punched Paulton in the jaw as he began stepping through. Shaw: Damn heelish tactics. Sheesh. Ump: What the hell is he doing. Oh no! He is NOT going to try this! Shaw: He has him locked in for a suplex. But there is only floor behind them. But Paulton is fighting it off. Ump: A couple shots to the gut. Shaw: I don't think Sharc noticed, but Fiend is back. Ump: Fiend is in the ring. Sharc gets up Paulton. Shaw: SPEAR! Ump: ALL THREE MEN FALL TO THE FLOOR IN A HEAP! "Holy shit! Holy shit!" the crowd roared out, with the move that took everyone out of their seats. Fiend speared Sharc, who was suplexing Kayden Paulton. The result? A human car wreck. Ump: This may now be a question of who can get up and into the ring first. Shaw: You think? Ump: Fiend should be in the best shape, but he did put all of his body into that spear. Shaw: And it looks like Paulton's going to make it up first. When the head is empty, things hurt less. Ump: All these guys are equally empty-headed in their own ways. And Sharc is next. Shaw: I guess Fiend is the smartest of the bunch. Though he is starting to move around. Sharc with a huge punch at Paulton, but it misses. Paulton kicks Sharc in the stomach. Uh oh! Ump: This may be it here! If Sharc feels In Color, this one is all over. He has Sharc up! Fiend is back in! He grabs Paulton from behind. Night Falls! Connects! Shaw: Good night Paulton. Ump: That was a modified version of Fiend's move, as Paulton and Sharc ended up both landing on him. But wait! Sharc's got the cover! On instinct, he hooks the legs! ONE! TWO! THREE! Ump: Sharc wins! Shaw: Unbelievable. What an opening match. It took Sharc long enough, but he finally finished off Paulton. Ump: Sharc gets his arm raised from the mat. All three of these men put on one hell of a show. I can't wait to see how the rest of this night unfolds. Outcome: Sharc via Pinfall. ---- Clothos. Clothos. November 3rd, 2000; Hostile Takeover The telephone is ringing, Disconnect the line, The tension is building, But I'm alright, Yeah, I'm alright. And the stars are colliding, So you might as well, OH, you might as well, Let me gooooooooo... Daniel Phillips, perhaps realizing that his partner was in trouble after Donnie Daze had just cost Joey Malone his spot in the North American Title tournament, managed to pull out a toaster from under the ring, and slid in to try and save him. However, Samuel Potright, the leader of the Misunderstood Family, grabbed him by the foot. Nikki: Potright grabs his foot! DAZE WITH A KNEEDROP TO PHILLIPS'S HEAD! Greg Parker: Daze has turned his back on his friends, the Winds of Change! Daze took the toaster from Phillips' hands, and positioned it dead center in the IWO ring, before he grabbed Daniel Phillips and gave him his inverted facebuster, the Dazed and Confused, dropping the not-quite-yet King of the Cruiserweights on the back of his head, on the toaster, at a sick angle. JT: He grabs Phillips from behind... OH MY GOD! DAZED AND CONFUSED ON THE TOASTER! Then he picked him up again and did it again, this time hitting at an even sicker angle than the last one. Blood trickled out of the back of his head as a result of this. JT: DAZE PICKS PHILLIPS UP AGAIN! AND HE DOES IT ONE MORE TIME! Nikki: My god! Phillips is bleeding! JT: The Misunderstood Family just took out Daniel Phillips! And he wouldn't set foot in an IWO ring as a competitor until a year and a half later. ---- Asleep at the Wheel. Asleep at the Wheel. Starring: Caden Ryder. Caden Ryder paces back and forth frustrated about A!W. He is dressed in his wrestling attire, his blue spandex wrestling pants and T-Shirt, just awaiting for a confrontation. Caden walks down the hallway a little more, spotting the big executive door that reads “Reed Young”. He stops walking, thinks to himself and decides to go in. Caden knocks on the door, but there is no answer. “Hello, anyone in there?” Asks Caden furiously pounding on the door, making it creak open. All the lights in the office are off and Caden looks for a switch, something snoring can be heard in the background. Caden turns on the light switch to the office, “Reed, what are you doing?” Reed Young is shown laying back in his executive chair sleeping, sleeping on the company’s most important night. “REED,” Caden screams. Reed bounces up from his chair, and starts arranging things on his desk, making them neat. He licks his hand and puts down a cow lick in his hair and asks, “ Hey Caden, has the show started yet?” Caden walks over to the chair in front of Young’s desk and takes a seat. Young: Well, Caden what can I do for you? Caden : Reed, I am sick of this, I want to be on TV more, I am “First-Rate” Caden Ryder, you can build a federation around the talent my body produces. But yet, I been in the federation for a month, and I have got no TV time, not one single segment, nothing. Young: So your trying to tell me that you want to be on TV? Caden: Yea, Reed, that’s what I am trying to get into your head. How can you not have someone with my talent not on shows, you pretty much pay me to sit in the locker room, hoping to show the fans what I can do. Young : Well, which TV would you like to be on? Caden: Argh! I don’t want to be on a single TV, I want to be on the shows this promotion produces, I want to feel like part of the team. Young: Yeah Team Action is pretty cool, well Caden I will see what I can do for you, but now I must get back to my sle… I mean running this promotion. I will do my best. Caden gets up and storms out of the office ready to kill. ---- Woodchuck Bill vs. The Ringer Woodchuck Bill vs. The Ringer Ump: Well folks we have had a great opening to the biggest show that Action Wrestling can put together! It can only get better from here on in and guess what is up next! Shaw: Yeah the Scout Camp match! That idiot Woodchuck Bill is going to get whipped around the ring by The Ringer! Ump: Take a look at the history between these two. (The scene switches to a highlight video package that starts out with Scout Master Scott walking down the ramp ready for his Submission Tournament match and then The Ringer doing some moves during the match and finally injuring and defeating Scott. The video goes on and showcases mostly the face to face encounters these two superstars have had and finally ends with The Ringer walking away from Bill shouting the one word that turned into an echo; Idiot, idiot....) Ump: Powerful stuff there-- (A marching army playing different musical instruments interrupt Liam as Bill’s Scout March theme plays.) Ump: Well would you look at that! A whole marching band is here just to introduce Woodchuck Bill! Shaw: Well after all it IS Juggernaut! Hoskins: The following contest is set up for one fall and is a Scout Camp Match! (Crowd cheers.) Hoskins: Introducing first, WOODCHUCK BILLLLLLL! (The music dies down a bit allowing two big saxophone players to take lead in the theme, then from the curtain Woodchuck Bill marches out! The fans cheered with impression of the big entrance as Bill trotted down the long ramp and into the ring.) Ump: Can you feel the electricity?! It’s off the scale! Woodchuck Bill is blowing the roof off! Shaw: Just wait till you hear all these people strong feed The Ringer with boos! That’ll be something! (Just as Derik finished his prediction the band finished to a crowd ovation.) Hoskins: And introducing his opponent.... (The crowd already laid in the boos and the “Idiot” chant.) Shaw: Ha you people don’t get it! If you cheer ‘idiot’ you’re only helping build The Ringer’s confidence! (Then the lights dimmed to a dark gray glow.... “Straight out of Line” By Godsmack cued in as the fans rustled in boos. The Ringer threw back the curtain and stood right at the top of the ramp way. Once the music got going The Ringer power walked down to the ring looking the best in shape in a while. The Ringer climbed a turnbuckle and raised both arms and swore at the crowd as the music faded.) Ump: We’re ready now! The Ringer has his usual red R on his trunks but for a change is there white tights instead of his usual black and Bill is in his Scout uniform! Wow I never thought I’d see him wrestle in that! Shaw: Look at The Ringer he’s walking right to the middle of the ring and confronting Bill like a man! DING DING DING! Ump: We are officially underway here! Shaw: Wait I don’t even know how a Scout Camp Match goes! Ump: Well I’ll tell you in just a minute, look at Bill and Ringer they haven’t moved from the middle of the ring since the bell rang. Shaw: The crowd seems to be restless, lets get it on! I’ve been waiting for this match for a while! Ump: It’ll be a dandy for sure. Shaw: Oh look at this! Bill puts his hand out to shake! Like The Ringer is going to fall for that! Ump: Whoa the Ringer just punched Bill in the face! Shaw: Lookout! Ump: Bill knocks down Ringer with a spear! What a retaliation! Shaw: Bill is gnawing away with mini punches! The Ringer is covering up but to no avail! Ump: The Ringer finally crawls away to a corner leaving Bill to rile up to crowd! Shaw: So Liam what can happen in a Scout Camp Match?! Ump: The Ringer gets up leaning in the corner, well Derik anything that can be used during a Scout Camp can be used and its pin only in the ring, remember that! Shaw: So a stove to the head is legal? Ump: You know it! Oh and by the way all the stuff is under the ring I say the ring crew put it there before the show. Shaw: Whoa so everything is at their disposal! Ump: Basically. Shaw: The Ringer makes a smart move by taking his time here in the early going. Bill seems too riled up! Ump: Well it is Bill’s first Juggernaut and here is a stat for you superstitious freaks out there, The Ringer is 0-1 at Juggernaut. That’s something to keep in mind as well. Shaw: Ringer and Bill grapple, hip toss to Bill! Whoa! What Impact! Ha! Look at this! The Ringer is already going outside! Ump: Uh oh! He’s grabbing a chair! He-- ummm, can’t! Shaw: Wh-What do you mean he can’t? Ump: The Ringer scoots back into the ring and measures Bill, but he can’t do this! WHACK!!!!!!!!!!!! Ump: A thunderous shot to the head! The ref could call it here! Shaw: WHAT THE HELL ARE YOU TALKING ABOUT? (The ref went over to Pat Hoskins and whispered something to him.) Hoskins: The ref has informed me that The Ringer has used an illegal object and will be warned the next time he does so he’ll be disqualified! (The camera turned and zoomed into The Ringer’s face which was filled with puzzlement.) Shaw: What? Why is a steel folding chair illegal? Ump: Because it isn’t on this list of usable weapons. Shaw: Okay now things are getting messed up! There’s a list of usable weapons?! Ump: Yes and instead of using a steel folding chair The Ringer has to use a folding wooden chair which can be found under the ring and that’s what I think the ref just told Ringer as he proceeds to the outside and under the ring. You can just imagine what’s running through The Ringer’s mind. Shaw: The Ringer is just now going through that tent bag and tossing things out. Ump: Hey look at all those pegs and tent poles! WHACK! WHACK! Ump: A bunch of tent poles delivered right to the back of Woodchuck Bill! Shaw: Woody is crying like an unwashed baby! I’m loving it! Ump: The Ringer seems to be enjoying this! But lookout Bill’s up and he hits a back suplex on Ringer! Shaw: It looks like The Ringer is in pain. Ump: Now its Bill’s chance to capitalize and look at him go, he’s actually setting the tent up! Shaw: Just like the goody good he is! Look at him! Right in the middle of a match Woody here is setting up a friggin tent! Ump: Woodchuck slides the poles in and erects the tent! I never thought I’d say this during a wrestling match but; the tent is pitched in the ring! Shaw: Bill picks up Ringer and is setting him up for the Demerit Points! Ump: Ringer gets tossed up, but lands on his feet! What a counter! Knee to the gut and elbow to the back of the head! Shaw: The Ringer slips outside. Ump: I think he’s scared of what might happen when he gets tossed into that pitched tent! Shaw: No, The Ringer’s going to toss Bill into the tent just you wait! Ump: Well Woodchuck isn’t waiting as he just nailed a dive over the ropes! The fans here are giving their appreciation to that spectacular move! Shaw: Wow Woody is already up! Duel entrance: Sunna’s “Power Struggle” blared through the arena, and while the theme was unfamiliar, the man who stepped out onto the ramp was not. Alex Lawson, better known as Duel, began making his way down to the ring, drawing a mixed reaction from the fans. Olivia Heimdal. Looking more beautiful and more vulnerable than ever. Finding himself unable to stop, he slowly altered his course and found himself standing closer to Olivia than he had since she slapped him. To his surprise, she didn’t wind up for another shot, and, in fact, she only recoiled slightly, which the big man wouldn’t have noticed if he wasn’t focused so intently on her. “Olivia, I…” but Duel stopped, sure that words wouldn’t be able to do his feelings justice. Livvie shook her head and looked up at him. He took this as a cue to listen. "You have no idea what I've been through up until this point." Alex shook his head. "No, I don't." "I've run the entire gambit in my head regarding you. I've wished you were dead, I've wished I was dead, I've wished that you and I had never happened. But you're still here, Alex." She kept his eye locked with hers. Lex had dealt with opponents with a less intimidating stare before. "So, here you are. I don't have any choice but to deal with you, do I?" Alex couldn't believe the words that came out of his mouth. "You're right." Livvie leaned closer to him. She looked confused. "What?" "I said you're right. You don't have a choice. You're going to have to deal with me from here on out, no matter what happens in there," Alex inclined his head toward the ring. "I know what I've done." Livvie's eyes widened. Lex suspected that she would have turned and fled had there not been throngs of people behind her. He guiltily used it to his advantage. "You're the most important one. Period. Ever. I know that I'm going in there to fight your brother and your best friend, but please, please don't hold that against me. We have to finish what was started. No flowers, no candle lit dinners, no stupid contrived love poetry written by someone else and twisted to fit someone else's romantic vendetta. She'd wanted to see him a wreck, in pain, beautiful naked sincerity shining through all the bravado he used as a front. She was angry at him for doing this to her. How did he know what to do? You’ll think of the damnedest stuff when you don’t really want to think about what’s really going on or what‘s about to happen. Rook liked this about how their relationship was until he realized that that was all it would ever be. He was a little in love with her, back in the day. After getting over the fact that they’d wind up killing each other if they ever went beyond friends, Rook settled into his role and was finally comfortable with it. “Rook!” she called as he approached, as if he wasn’t going to stop for her. He knew that if he did, he’d wind up planted in that spot, and he’d never get this over with. == Frank’s entrance: Frank listened to his music for the first few measures before walking through the curtain. He wasn’t doing it to be dramatic; he was scared. The things he’d gotten himself into over the years because of people and relationships was on his mind. Was this the worst situation to date? That was questionable, actually. The physical pain was always easier to tolerate than the emotional no matter what. Frank knew this to be true in regards to himself. Especially after being punched repeatedly, beaten up (but not necessarily down). The fights always found him regardless of whether he was looking for them or not. So, you learned how to fight back. You learned what the signs were. Impending doom and impending black eyes became normal. And after a while, you learned to like what happened to you. And the impending doom of a physical altercation was so much easier to chart than that of emotional doom. That’s why he did this. This could be defined, this could be categorized, easier all around. Not the smartest way to solve things, but sometimes this was all you could do. This was all he could do at this point. He’d done his best. And that wasn’t good enough. And even if it was good enough, they just hadn’t wanted to listen. Or maybe they did…fuck. This wasn’t the easiest thing to define which kind of sucked because Frank was looking for a little more definition. Or for things to make more sense. Or a person who was in possession of more answers than Frank was. Little Livvie-Lou, short for Olivia Louise, clutching the safety barricade, her knuckles white. A casualty of all the shit Frank had been through because she wanted to be. Frank’s fan club, support group, drinking buddy, sympathetic ear when she wasn’t going through her own personal bullshit. His sister, one of the few on the planet to tell it like it was. He went to her and put his hand over hers. He felt the knuckles relax underneath his palm. She leaned in, got in his face, and said “You know, Frank, it isn’t very nice to worry the people who love you.” Frank’s brow furrowed. This was how she felt? Didn’t she see? Of course she didn’t. She wasn’t ignorant, her waters had just been muddied. Frank clarified things for her. “I know. Why do you think I’m doing this?” Livvie leaned back, and Frank knew she got it just by the look on her face. She had to have been on the verge of tears for most of the evening because two steady streams flowed from her eyes now. She got it. Ump: I guess. ---- KroW vs. 'Showcase' Sean Cylear KroW vs. 'Showcase' Sean Cylear At least the time had come to end the ongoing war between two men. A war that had lasted nearly three years would finally come to an end. A hatred that had only blossomed more and more with every winking second of time would finally be unleashed into a brutal battle. Two men with so much rage against one another would finally meet face to face inside of a steel cage. One man nearly had his career ended, while the other almost had his life ended. It was a war, not between two wrestlers, but between two men. After all the months, the years, of a battle brewing…the war would finally end in one last bout. Deafening cheers came forth from the crowd as the lights suddenly dimmed allowing flashes of light bulbs to shine upon the lowering cage. The steel structure was made of pure metal and nothing more. There would be no give to the structure at all. If a man was to meet this steel, he would feel a great amount of pain. As the cage was slowly lowered to the squared circle, attendants at ringside rushed to the ring to secure the cage to the ring, making sure all of the action would stay inside. All looked on in amazement as the structure was laid before them, a mere few feet away. But the most important thing about the cage was the top. The top was sealed shut, there would be no escaping this match; it would all remain in the steel cage. As the cage was being secured, Pat Hoskins was quick to stand up from his seat and bring the microphone to his mouth. Pat Hoskins – Ladies and Gentlemen, the next contest is scheduled for one fall and is a Steel Cage Match. The rules of this contest are simple: there are no disqualifications, no count outs, and obviously no way of escaping the steel cage. The only way you can defeat your opponent is by forcing your opponent to submit in the ring, or to cover your opponent for the respected three fall count. And with that ladies and gentlemen, allow me to introduce the FIRST contestant. The fans anticipation only grew after the announcement, and that anticipation would soon turn into a deafening amount of cheers, immediately when the arena went pitch black. The fans went wild as flames shot from the entrance ramp and the name ‘KroW’ appeared on the Action Tron. The roof in Madison Square Garden nearly blew off as “It Gets Me Through” blasted across the New York City crowd. Lights bulbs flashed wildly, as a red light shined upon the entrance ramp, revealing the figure of the Satanic God. KroW stood perfectly still at the entrance ramp for a few moments, his head lowered and his eyes shut; almost soaking up the fan fare. He wasn’t used to being cheered, he never got used to it, but it was something he grew to like. But the fans were the last thing on his mind. All that was on his mind was Sean Cylear and how to destroy him. KroW looked up in a blink of an eye as more flames shot vertically from the entrance ramp. Even with all the pyrotechnics and the cheering of the fans; the only thing that really remained in KroW’s mind was Sean Cylear and how to destroy him. KroW reached the ring side area and took a look at the steel structure that lay before him. He grabbed the steel links and shook them a bit, testing the stability of the cage. His eyes traveled all across the steel cage, looking at every corner, every chain link, everything on the cage itself as a weapon to use. KroW looked over his left shoulder, and than his right, staring as the fans cheered wildly for the Satanic God. He brushed past them and ordered the official to open the cage door. Once open, KroW used to steps to enter the cage via the door. Once in the cage, with lights continuing to flash, KroW stood on the second rope and held out both arms, and roared out. The fans responded to this with a huge amount of cheers. But the lights returned, and the flames calmed to nothing but then smoke. KroW leaped off the second rope and began to check the flexibility of the ring ropes by tugging on them as hard as he could, as the music of “It Gets Me Through” died down. KroW stared forward at the entrance ramp, his eyes glued to the curtains that would soon be pushed aside to reveal his opponent; a man who faked his own death, a man who had been hiding from KroW for too long, and a man who would have to face his devil tonight. Pat Hoskins – And his opponent, he hails from Sacramento, and weighs in at two hundred and thirty seven pounds. He is a former Action Champion; he is the Showcase Sean Cylear! Immediately after “Arc Arsenal” came to the PA system the fans jeered their hearts out. Sean Cylear busted through the curtain with a grin plastered over his face as he greeted the fans with a few middle fingers and cocky attitude. Sean Cylear seemed unworried about the match at hand, until he actually looked in the ring. The grin that was plastered over his face quickly disappeared the moment he met with KroW’s piercing stare. KroW was now dead still in his vertical position, as he stared directly into the frightened eyes of Sean Cylear. Cylear only gave one last glance at KroW before attempting to ignore his gaze by looking out at the angry crowd. The official opened the cell door for Cylear, but Cylear simply blew past the official and began to circle the ring side area, but being very cautious when rounding KroW, who didn’t stop his stare. Sean Cylear’s eyes told the story, the story of how frightened he was to enter this kind of match. He was forced into a war with the Satanic God, he was forced to sign him back to Action, and he was forced to drop the bounty on his head; Cylear was considerably the bitch in the equation. You would expect some sort of rage to erupt in Cylear, but fear was too powerful and outweighed the rage. He again past by the cell door again, looking at the ground as he began to speak to himself. ”You can do this Sean, you can kick this guy’s ass,” Cylear repeated to himself over and over again, as he continued to stretch out his muscles and crack a few bones. The official at ringside was getting a bit impatient and ordered Cylear to enter the cage. Cylear simply flipped the official off and walked over to the ring side attendant’s area. He grabbed Pat Hoskins by his collar and hurled him to the side, as he grabbed the steel chair and folded it up. The fans immediately protested the use of the steel chair, as did the official at the door, as he argued with Cylear to put it away. Ump: First of all, he’s got to get into the cage, this is bullshit. He’s too scared to get into the ring with KroW. Shaw: Cylear never even started this fight, what the hell does KroW have against him anyway? The theme music had died down, and all that was left was to get Cylear in the ring. KroW attempted to remain patient, but his patience was wearing thin as Cylear continued to argue with the outside official. The in-ring official continually ordered Cylear to put the chair down as well, but Cylear insisted on bringing it in. The Satanic God closed his eyes and punched the cage wall, before stomping towards the door. The fans cheered with every step KroW took. On the outside, the official had finally snatched the chair from Cylear’s grasp. Cylear seemed to be angry, but out of the corner of his eye, he noticed the Satanic God stomping towards the cage door. KroW poked his head from underneath the top rope and looked to exit the ring when suddenly… CLANK! Ump: That son of a bitch! That no good son of a bitch! Shaw: Yeah! Yeah see Liam? Now THAT is skill! The fans cringed at the site, as Cylear slammed the cell door into KroW’s skull. KroW’s entire body was sent back, as a trail of warm blood slowly began to come from the Satanic God’s skull. KroW held onto his forehead in pain, as Cylear arrogantly smiled at the crowd and entered the ring. The official on the outside, Jason Johnson, simply snarled in disgust as he closed the cage door behind Cylear and locked the door shut. The bell had rung. Cylear arrogantly strutted towards the downed KroW, as the Satanic God fought to get to his feet. KroW finally got to one knee, but was immediately sent back down to the mat with a boot to the side of the head. LEGEND CHAMPION HERO THE FOURTH MAN... coming SOON... ---- 'The Original' Jeff Garvin (B) vs. Coral Avalon 'The Original' Jeff Garvin (B) vs. Coral Avalon Back after a short break between matches, the cameras once again focused on the special Juggernaut entrance stage. The New York crowd was excited for this next contest, a match-up between two of the great young stars in Action! Two that have quite the history with one another. This was actually the third time they would meet one-on-one in front of a pay per view audience. The first time- at Retaliation '02, where Jeff Garvin made Coral Avalon tap out in his signature finishing hold, the Hammer Jammer. Then again at the famous Players & Pawns event, competing in an Iron Man match, which would go on to win the Match of the Year award, Coral would come out victorious. However, both times there was nothing but pride on the line... Tonight, on the biggest stage of the year, Jeff Garvin will defend his beloved Bantam Championship. The two rivals are one-and-one against each other, leaving the fans wondering who actually is the best between them. Being the third, and what could be the final encounter, this would be the decisive victory that would hold the bragging rights. Allowing either Jeff or Coral the right to say, they really are the better man. Tension had been building since August of last year, and in moments... everything, all the verbal jabs, all the physical confrontation, the hatred even, would come to a head. In the instant it took for the technical crew to cue up Fuel's 'Won't Back Down', the crowd was on their feet, cheering for Coral Avalon and awaiting his entrance. Ump: How many times have we seen these two go one-on-one? They have created some of the finest pure wrestling matches we have ever seen, but it has never been this personal or this important... This is the biggest match of their careers up to this point. Shaw: Without a doubt, and if Coral hadn't snuck in that victory at Players & Pawns, there would be no need to even HAVE this match! His victory over Jeff MADE him a contender, it made him something special... This was his first big match back after sustaining a pulled muscle in his arm at the hands of Daniel Phillips, and though he did indeed make it through the 'W101 Gauntlet', defeating KSZ and Chris Chambers in one night, many were still skepticle as to whether Coral had any ring rust in him. Ump: Well, I agree that that match probably helped his career along, but in no way would Coral still not be at this level. It's simple- Coral is here because his deserves to be here. The kid has worked harder than almost anyone on the roster, he's smaller then most... that makes him an underdog, but you'll never hear Avalon make excuses. As Coral walked from the back, he rose a fist in the air and smiled at the fans in attendance. They responded with a huge 'pop'. Then, walking down the long steel ramp, the smile was wiped clean off his face. The high he got from the fans' reaction was all but gone, it was time to focus. Shaw: Heh! The best way to avoid this position was to interlock one's hands together, preventing the opponent from hyperextending the elbow.
